Every cipher run carries a contemporary interlinear decipherment, and the key is bound as f. 211 ([`targets/lodi1584/`](targets/lodi1584/)).
- **110 — Sr. Fra. Poynts (Hispania) to unknown recipient, 28 Jul 1527, BL Cotton MS Vespasian C IV ff. 182–185 (DECODE R8567)**: read at the time, 21 Sept 2026. A clear English despatch of Edward Lee and Sir Francis Poyntz to Wolsey (July 1527); its only cipher, three short runs on f. 184v, carries the contemporary decipherment above each run (*it can not be so well bestowed…*), and Tomokiyo's Lee–Worcester key on the record fits. See [targets/poyntz1527/](targets/poyntz1527/), removed 21 Sept 2026.
- **242 — The bishop of Bergamo and the archbishop of Nazareth (France) to The secretariat, 3 ciphertexts, AAV Segr. Stato Francia 18 (DECODE R15–R17)**: read, 21 Sept 2026. R15 read before by Lasry; R16 (whose DECODE decryption is R15's) read with Lasry's key + Meister p. 324; R17 read with Meister no. 40 (moved to [targets/morosini1588/](targets/morosini1588/) on 14 Oct 2026, one key per entry). See [targets/bergamo1585/](targets/bergamo1585/). [Write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/bergamo1585.html)
- **241 — The bishops of Rimini and Bergamo (France) to The secretariat, 2 ciphertexts, ASV Segr. Stato Francia 17 (DECODE R13–R14)**: resolved 21 Sept 2026. R13 (f. 30r) was read at the time: its short cipher passage carries an interlinear decipherment (*che è portato da Mons. di Bellièvre, già stato ugonotto et hor forse atheo, in casa…*), the plaintext DECODE's partial key was built from. R14 is not a cipher: it is the clear subject index of the volume ("Anno 84, Volume 140, Francia"). See [targets/damiata1624/](targets/damiata1624/), removed.
- **258 — Nuncio Salviati, archbishop of Nazareth (France) to The secretariat, ASV Segr. Stato Francia 7 (DECODE R12)**: read at the time, 21 Sept 2026. The 1574 letter (f. 249) carries the decipherment written above each cipher run (*Mons. il Duca…*, *la Regina…*); Lasry's F6 key is on the record. See [targets/damiata1624/](targets/damiata1624/), removed.
- **251 — The Secretariat (Spain) to The Nunciature, 10 ciphertexts, ASV Segr. Stato Spagna 423 (DECODE R179–R188)**: found already solved, 21 Sept 2026. George Lasry's key "ASV - S423" and decipherment (Oct 2020) are on every record; his key values no nomenclator group, and 46 of the 166 were recovered here from context (90.6% → 95.3% read). See [targets/nunzio1736/](targets/nunzio1736/).
- **257 — French Nuncio (France) to The Secretariat, 10 ciphertexts, ASV Segr. Stato Francia 64 (DECODE R62–R71)**: already read. Norbert Biermann broke the key in 2018 (from 64/10, R71) and decryptions of all ten are on the DECODE records (codebreaker.py, 64–77 % in the Italian LM); DECODE's "Non-decrypted" status is stale. See [targets/damiata1624/](targets/damiata1624/), removed 21 Sept 2026.
- **252 — The nuncio (France) to The secretariat, ASV Segr. Stato Francia 6 (DECODE R11)**: already read. Lasry's reconstructed F6 key and a full decryption (D3185) are on the DECODE record. Removed 21 Sept 2026.
- **255 — Bernardino Naro (France) to The secretariat, ASV Segr. Stato (DECODE R59)**: read at the time, 21 Sept 2026. Naro, Paris, 14 April 1625 (not 3 Jan); three short cipher passages glossed on the leaf, and the Biermann–Bosbach 1625 key on the DECODE records fits them. See [targets/damiata1624/](targets/damiata1624/), removed 21 Sept 2026.
- **232 — C. A. graaf van Rechteren (St Petersburg) to the States General, 4 Oct 1785, NA 1.01.02 inv. 7407 (DECODE R1039)**: read at the time, 21 Sept 2026. Images 5448–5449 are the Griffie's decipherment ("Ont cyfferde Missive"), catalogued by DECODE as cleartext. See [targets/rechteren1785/](targets/rechteren1785/).
- **256 — Nuncio Damiata (France) to The Secretariat, 2 ciphertexts, ASV Segr. Stato (DECODE R60–R61)**: read at the time, 21 Sept 2026. R60 is Bernardino Spada, archbishop of Damiata, Paris 15 Aug 1625; R61 is Bernardino Naro, Paris, last Feb 1625. Each short cipher passage has its decipherment written above it, and the Biermann–Bosbach 1625 key on the DECODE records fits. See [targets/damiata1624/](targets/damiata1624/), removed 21 Sept 2026.
- **82 — Sir Francis Walsingham (Nonsuch) to Edward Wotton, ambassador to Scotland, 4 ciphertexts, BL Add MS 32657 (DECODE R4838–R4840, R4844)**: read in part, 21 Sept 2026. Clear letters with a two-digit name code; 28 Jul and 7 Aug in CSP Scotland viii (nos. 40, 65); 21 Aug and 10 Sep transcribed; about fourteen sign-cipher words of 10 Sep unread. See [targets/walsingham1585/](targets/walsingham1585/). [Write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/walsingham1585.html)
- **47 — Constantinople envoys to Ferenc Rákóczi II, 1706, MNL OL G15 Caps. C Fasc. 36, pp. 18–21 (DECODE R793–R796)**: read 21 Sept 2026 with Pápai's envoy key (DECODE R580/R581), unchanged: four despatches of János Pápai, 1 Jan–Mar 1706, 5,012 of 5,043 groups (groups above 322 are nulls). See [targets/papai1706/](targets/papai1706/), removed 21 Sept 2026. [Write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/papai1706.html).
- **50 — Unknown sender to Ferenc Rákóczi II, 1707, MNL OL G15 Caps. D Fasc. 80, pp. 110–111 (DECODE R784)**: read 21 Sept 2026 with Pápai's envoy key (DECODE R580/R581), 587 of 594 groups; an unsigned Constantinople report on the French ambassador and the Porte. See [targets/papai1706/](targets/papai1706/), removed 21 Sept 2026. [Write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/papai1706.html).
- **81 — Edward Wotton (Scotland) to Sir Francis Walsingham, 3 ciphertexts, BL Add MS 32657 (DECODE R4841–R4843)**: read. Wotton's own drafts of 25 Aug, 30 Aug and 1 Sep 1585 in clear English with a numeric name code; 19 Arran, 39 Master of Gray, 10 King James, four more probable, 27, 32, 36, 40 open. See [targets/wotton1585/](targets/wotton1585/), removed 21 Sept 2026. [Write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/wotton1585.html).
- **71 — King Charles I (Oxford) to Prince Rupert, BL Add MS 18983 f. 14 (DECODE R4921)**: read 21 Sept 2026 with Lasry's King–Queen key SP106-5 (R929); the artillery train and Rupert's march. [Write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/rupert1645.html).
- **199 — Charles V (Burgos) to Isabella of Portugal / Prince Philip, 2 ciphertexts, AGS Estado legs. 496 and 59 (DECODE R9952, R9957)**: already read. Two unrelated letters. R9957 is Charles V to the Empress, Borgo San Donnino (not Burgos), 17 May 1536. It was deciphered and printed by Luo (2021, p. 287) with the key AGS Estado leg. 1.1.1 doc. 169, and its pp. 16–17 cipher is also deciphered on the leaves. R9952 is Charles V to Prince Philip, Cremona, 19 June 1543, deciphered at the time in the margins and on ff. 13–14 (the Farnese offer for Milan). Nothing new to read. See [targets/charles1536/](targets/charles1536/), removed 21 Sept 2026.
- **246 — Abbot Vincenti and nuncio Lucini (Spain) to The Secretariat, ASV Segr. Stato Spagna 304 (DECODE R120)**: a clear letter of 13 Oct 1767 whose last sentence is in cipher; read with Lasry's key on the record, 21 Sept 2026. Code 8107 open. [Write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/lucini1767.html).

- **245 — Prospero S Croce (France) to Cardinale del Monte, 5 ciphertexts, ASV Segr. Stato, Francia 3 (DECODE R6–R10)**: read at the time and in print. The letters date from 26 Mar to 23 Dec 1553 (DECODE's 28 Jul 1552 is the start of the volume). The decifrati are bound in the volume, and all five letters are printed deciphered in Lestocquoy, *Correspondance du nonce en France Prospero Santa Croce* (Acta Nuntiaturae Gallicae 9, 1972), nos. 67, 71, 112, 124 and the second letter of 23 Dec 1553. Lasry's key has been on the records since 2020. R9's three struck-through cipher lines are the clear postscript written below them. Nothing new to read. See [targets/santacroce1552/](targets/santacroce1552/), removed 21 Sept 2026.
- **217 — Van Reede (Berlin) to William V, 4 Feb 1792, KHA Prins Willem V inv. 205 (DECODE R1057)**: read, 21 Sept 2026, with the Grand Chiffre key R1024. A cover note: lemon-juice writing on the enclosure, then 21 declared nulls. [Write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/vanreede1792.html)
- **89 — Sr. Thomas Smith to Secretary Cecil / The Queen, 6 ciphertexts, BL Add MS 4136 (DECODE R9248–R9254)**: read at the time (SP 70 originals deciphered; 1563 letters printed by Forbes 1741). The six records hold 18 letters, 1563–66; Smith's key is Add MS 4136 f. 179 (DECODE R9261). Read here with it, 21 Sept 2026. [Write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/smith1562.html).
- **43 — Ferdinand I (Augsburg) to Johann Malvezzi, 23 Jan 1548, Military History Archive 1548/3 (DECODE R366)**: read in part, 21 Sept 2026. The key was filed on DECODE at the same shelfmark as a separate record (R367; second copy R386). The ciphered passages carry the French attempts to break the Habsburg–Ottoman peace and the envoy's pay (4,300 ducats by the secretary Justus de Argento, credit of 3,000 more at Venice); short stretches on pp. 1, 6 and 7 open. [Write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/malvezzi1548.html).
- **83 — "... (England) to Lorde Regent of Scotlande", 26 Jul 1569, BL Add MS 33531 ff. 79–80 (DECODE R8348)**: read at the time. Sir Nicholas Throckmorton to the Regent Moray, Greenwich, 20 July 1569 (DECODE's 26 July is a slip); a contemporary interlinear decipherment covers every cipher line, Bain calendars it (*CSP Scotland* ii no. 1103, "in cipher deciphered") and Tomokiyo has the key. Nothing new to read. See [targets/throck1569/](targets/throck1569/), removed 21 Sept 2026.
- **131 — Cobham (Belgia) to Walsingham, 4 ciphertexts, 1588, BL Harley MS 287 (DECODE R8490–R8496)**: read in part, 21 Sept 2026. Key rebuilt from the interlinear glosses of Cobham's April letters; code numbers glossed; about a third of the cipher words read. [Write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/cobham1588.html).
- **155 — Giovanni Battista Bonzagni (Eger) to Ippolito I d'Este, 1512–14, ASMo Ambasciatori b. 4/12, DECODE R1123–R1125**: read at the time. R1123 (no. 4) and R1124 (no. 6) carry contemporary interlinear decipherments of every cipher passage; R1125 (no. 12) has a decipherment slip filed with it (DECODE image 5), which fits the cipher block of p. 3 word for word under a simple sign substitution. Nothing new to read. See [targets/bonzagni1512/](targets/bonzagni1512/), removed 21 Sept 2026.
- **150 — Charles Poupet de Lachaulx to Charles I, 1522, BNE MSS/20212/39/1-2, DECODE R1187**: read at the time; the contemporary decipherment is bound in (39/2); French letter, Vitoria 28 May 1522. [Write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/poupet1522.html), removed 21 Sept 2026.
- **147 — "Alonso Sánchez (Venice) to unknown recipient", 10 ciphertexts, 1523, RAH Signatura 9/28 (DECODE R9670–R9770)**: despatches to Charles V; already calendared (Bergenroth, CSP Spain 2, nos. 549, 552, 554, 558) from contemporary decipherings; 1522 key unchanged; the uncalendared letter of 16 Aug 1523 (R9767) read in part, 21 Sept 2026. See [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/sanchez1523.html).
- **44 — Ferenc Rákóczi II to unknown recipient, 4 ciphertexts, MNL OL (DECODE R483, R852, R912, R922)**: read, 21 Sept 2026. Three are French reports *to* Rákóczi read with key DECODE R639 (corrected) or a clear copy; R483 (Latin, Lwów 27 June 1711, another key) was deciphered interlinearly at the time. See [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/rakoczi1704.html); R483 since 14 Oct 2026 has its own [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/rakoczi1711.html).
- **222 — Wolff to Wilhelmina, Princess of Prussia, 25 Jul 1801, KHA Wilhelmina Prinses van Pruisen inv. 192 (DECODE R2232)**: read, 21 Sept 2026, with the key R2233 Wolff sent on 8 Sept 1801. See [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/r2232.html).
- **221 — idem (unknown) to Prince William V, 17 Aug 1796, KHA Prins Willem V inv. 339 (DECODE R2234)**: read, 21 Sept 2026, with the five-alphabet key R2235, one alphabet per line. See [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/r2234.html).
- **229 — Cornelis Haga (Constantinople) to the States General, 2 ciphertexts, 1 Feb – 4 May 1620, NA The Hague, 1.01.02 (Staten-Generaal), inv. nr. 6894 (DECODE R2113, R2115)**: read with the key R2118 from the same inventory number, 21 Sept 2026. The two records hold four letters, not two. See [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/haga1620.html), removed 21 Sept 2026.
- **132 — "Unknown sender to unknown recipient", 7 ciphertexts, 20–22 Mar 1587, BL Harley MS 287 (DECODE R8477–R8487)**: Lord Cobham at Ostend to Walsingham, 20 and 22 March 1587/8; read, 21 Sept 2026. See [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/harley287.html). R8477 (f. 11) is a different letter and code, not read.
- **96 — Throckmorton to Secretary Cecil; Lords of the Council, 8–20 Feb 1560, BL, Add MS 4136 ff. 100-101 (DECODE R3026)**: closed: already in print (read at the time), 21 Sept 2026. Two letters: to Cecil, 8 Feb 1559/60 (CSP For. ii 706; Forbes, *Full View* i 324-25) and to the Lords of the Council, Blois 20 Feb 1559/60 (CSP For. ii 751; Forbes i 328), both originals "portions in cipher, deciphered". DECODE has no images, so the ciphertext was not seen. Notes only (targets/throck1560/NOTES.md), removed 21 Sept 2026.
- **95 — Mr. Lomer to Secretary Cecil, Dec 1563, BL, Add MS 4136 f 138-139 (DECODE R9235)**: closed: deciphered at the time, 21 Sept 2026. "Lomer" is John Somer; the letter (Paris, 9 Dec 1563) is calendared as deciphered in CSP Foreign vi no. 1471. The key is Throckmorton's third cipher (R9262), checked on passage (1) ("from the French ambassador … advertised the same"). The record also holds the end of a Throckmorton despatch and a Smith passage. Notes only (targets/lomer1563/NOTES.md), removed 21 Sept 2026.
- **91 — Mr. Middelmore to Secretary Cecil, 2 ciphertexts, 19–26 Jul 1563, BL, Add MS 4136 (DECODE R9246, R9247)**: closed: already in print, 21 Sept 2026. Both letters (Henry Middelmore, Rouen 19 July and Vallemont 26 July 1563) are printed in full, deciphered, in Forbes, *Full View* ii 470-75 and 490-92; a simple substitution with code signs (∞ = Newhaven), checked on the 26 July passages. R9247 duplicates R9246 image 3. Notes only (targets/middelmore1563/NOTES.md), removed 21 Sept 2026.
- **85 — William Cecil, 1st Baron Burghley to Sir Ralph Sadler and Sir James Croft, 2 ciphertexts, 11 Sep – 30 Oct 1559, BL, Add MS 33591 (DECODE R4847, R4860)**: closed: already in print, 21 Sept 2026. Both letters are printed in Clifford, *Sadler State Papers* I (1809), nos. XXXVI and XCVII; the only cipher is two groups in the 11 Sept letter (② = the Earl of Arran, “+ H oo” = 200 crowns), and the 30 Oct letter is in clear. Notes only (targets/burghley1559/NOTES.md), removed 21 Sept 2026.
- **208, 209 — Sigismund Heusner von Wandersleben (Kassel) to Axel Oxenstierna, 1637, Riksarkivet, DECODE R4332 and R3816 (one letter, same scans)**: already read by Waldispühl & Kopal 2024. See [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/heusner1637.html), removed 21 Sept 2026.
- **231 — Arend Willem baron van Reede (1747-1615) (Berlin) to Grand Pensionary L.P. van den Spiegel  1736-1800)_ / Greffier Hendrik Fagel jr. (1765-1838) and Secret Committee of the States General, 3 ciphertexts, 29 Dec 1787 – 24 Mar 1793, NA The Hague, Laurens Pieter van de Spiegel, inv.nr. 190 (3 items)**: R1026 and R1027 read in part with the Grand Chiffre R1024. See [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/vanreede1787.html), removed 21 Sept 2026. R1893 (24 Mar 1793), in a different code, was restored on 14 Oct 2026 as an open entry under the same id (attempted, not solved: [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/vanreede1793.html)).
- **154 — Nicolo Sadoleto (Pozsony) to Ferdinand I / Ercole I d'Este, 4 ciphertexts, 15 Jul 1482 – 27 Nov 1482, ASMo, Ambasciatori b. 1/9, Envoy reports, Ferrara (4 items)**: read in part. See [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/sadoleto1482.html), removed 21 Sept 2026.
- **48 — Philippe Groffey (?) to Ferenc Rákóczi II (Pompeio Cesoni), 15 Oct 1707, MNL OL, G15 Caps. C. Fasc. 39. 277-279**: Cipher identified; report read in substance; exact edition open.. See [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/rakoczi1707.html), removed 21 Sept 2026.
- **31 — Intercepted enemy cipher letters summarised for Nevers, Sep–Oct 1589, BnF fr. 3977 no. 96 (the summary); intercepts to be located**: closed: no ciphertext. See [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/nevers1589.html), removed 21 Sept 2026.
- **11 — Catherine de Médicis and the Court to the bishop of Rennes, 31 Jul 1563; 1560s, BnF fr. 3181 f. 55; 500 Colbert 390 pp. 139, 357; 500 Colbert 392 p. 231**: read in part. See [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/rennes1563.html), removed 21 Sept 2026.
- **29 — Letter to the abbé d'Orbais (Cardinal Pellevé's secretary, Rome → Jean de Piles, League council in Paris), 23 Aug 1589, BnF fr. 3413 no. 62**: read in part, 18 Sept 2026; none of the three bound keys fits, the cipher signs read with Tomokiyo's Nevers–Piles alphabet, about 30 signs open. See [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/orbais.html), removed 21 Sept 2026.
- **146 — Luis Fernández? (Sesa) to unknown recipient, 1523, RAH 9/27, DECODE R9660–R9666**: resolved, 21 Sept 2026. The Duke of Sessa to Charles V, 20 Feb–27 Apr 1523 (Salazar A-27); four are letters or duplicates calendared by Bergenroth (CSP Spain vol. 2 nos. 534, 540, 541, 544) from contemporary decipherments, no. 545 is clear; the cipher is the sessa1524 nomenclator, and the uncalendared 20 Feb letter was read in part with that key. [Write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/sessa1523.html)
- **220 — “unkmown (Germany) to Prince William V”, 1 Jan 1795, KHA Prins Willem V inv. 339, DECODE R1892**: read in part, 21 Sept 2026. A 6×6 digit square broken ciphertext-only; a son of William V to an officer of the émigré troops, c. 1795; ~30 word signs open. [Write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/r1892.html), removed 21 Sept 2026.
- **68 — Leopold Viktorin von Windischgrätz (Brussels) to Ernst Friedrich von Windischgrätz, 18 Nov 1721, SOA Plzeň RA Windischgrätz inv. 1433, DECODE R5029**: read in part, 21 Sept 2026, with Jakub Mírka's reconstructed key (2023) attached to the DECODE record; every letter-spelled passage read, 23 nomenclator codes open. See [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/windischgraetz1721.html), removed 21 Sept 2026.
- **244 — The Secretariat (Cardinal of Como) to the nuncio in France (Dandini), 1580, ASV Segr. Stato Francia 283C, DECODE R72–R73**: read, 21 Sept 2026. R73 decrypted by George Lasry on DECODE (2020); R72 uses a different letter key in the same frame, rebuilt here and matching the contemporary decipherment on f. 52. Split 14 Oct 2026 by key: [R72 write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/dandini1580.html), [R73 write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/dandini1580r73.html)
- **191 — Louis XIII to Melchior de Sabran (Genoa) and to Brûlart de Léon (Venice), 1615–1631, BnF Baluze 155 f. 79, fr. 18043 f. 83, fr. 18044 f. 109, DECODE R2748, R2785, R2786**: already solved by others. R2748 (Dijon, 28 March 1631) was solved by George Lasry in 2022, key on Tomokiyo's cryptiana GL.htm (same cipher in Sabran's letters, fr. 4134–4135); R2785 and R2786 were read by Satoshi Tomokiyo with his reconstructed key (louisxiii.htm), the few unread symbols probably nulls. Removed 21 Sept 2026; split 14 Oct 2026 into `targets/sabran1631/` (merged 22 Oct 2026 into `targets/sabran1633/`, [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/sabran1633.html)) and `targets/brulart1615/` ([write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/brulart1615.html)), two different ciphers.
- **192 — Odoardo Farnese, Duke of Parma, to Sabran, 27 May 1637, BnF Baluze 156 f. 40, DECODE R2753**: solved by George Lasry in 2022, key on cryptiana GL.htm. Found while checking 191; not viewed here. Removed 21 Sept 2026.

- **46 — János Pápai (Constantinople) to Ferenc Rákóczi II, 1706–1710, DECODE R731–R823**: read in part, 21 Sept 2026. The key issued to Pápai (DECODE R580) reads six of the seven letters, 98.5% of groups; R731's graphic-sign passages stay open. See [targets/papai1706/](targets/papai1706/), removed 21 Sept 2026. R731, a different cipher, was restored on 14 Oct 2026 as an open entry under the same id (attempted, not solved: [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/papai1710.html)).
- **228 — Etienne Bourdeaux (Berlin) to Maarten van der Goes, 31 January 1801, DECODE R1944**: read, 21 Sept 2026, from the contemporary clear copy imaged as R1946 image 3 (fol. 295). [Write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/r1944.html), removed 21 Sept 2026.
- **180 — “Unknown sender (Rome) to Anne de Montmorency”, eight ciphertexts, BnF Français 3053, DECODE R4233–R4248**: the sender is the cardinal de Mâcon, ambassador to Paul III; seven records read in part with the Tomokiyo/Lasry key, R4248 resolved by the contemporary decipherment on f. 86. See [targets/rome1536/](targets/rome1536/), removed 21 Sept 2026; R4248 is its own entry since 14 Oct 2026, [targets/r4248/](targets/r4248/).
- **37 and 41 — Bernardo de Salinas (London) and Pedro Ronquillo (Nijmegen) to Baltasar de Fuenmayor, 1676–78, DECODE R966–R975 (and R976–R984, R1001 from the same volume)**: read, 21 Sept 2026, with the Balbases 1677 key, which fits unchanged; four letters with no margin read in gist. See [targets/ronquillo1676/](targets/ronquillo1676/), removed 21 Sept 2026.
- **260 — Antonio Sauli and Riario (Portugal) to the Secretariat, 1579–81, ASV Segr. Stato Portogallo 8, DECODE R190–R194**: read, 21 Sept 2026, from the contemporary interlinear decipherments checked with George Lasry's reconstructed key; five code words added. See `targets/sauli1579/`.
- **63 — "King Charles I to William Boswell", 1628, TNA SP 106/5, DECODE R413**: already read by Mark Woodard on DECODE (2021), a monoalphabetic newsletter; the sender is Sir Ralph Boswell and the date 15 Dec 1627. Written up 21 Sept 2026 ([boswell1628](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/boswell1628.html)).

- **42 — Conde de Hernán Núñez (Stockholm) to Baltasar de Fuenmayor, December 1674, DECODE R1012–R1015**: read, 21 Sept 2026. The marginal decipherments are verified with the Balbases 1677 key, which fits unchanged. See [targets/hernannunez1674/](targets/hernannunez1674/), removed 21 Sept 2026.
- **23 — Henri IV to Savary de Brèves (Constantinople), 1603, BnF fr. 3541 ff. 60-104**: plaintext known. The original key is BnF fr. 3462 f. 103. Berger de Xivrey, *Lettres missives* VI (1853), prints three of the twelve letters in full from a contemporary clear copy and summarises the other nine; a second clear copy of all twelve is BnF fr. 4029 ff. 118-128. Only a letter-by-letter check of the nine summarised letters is left, and it waits on BnF images. [Write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/breves1603.html), removed 21 Sept 2026.

- **261 — Cardinal Alessandrino to the nuncio in Spain, 1567 (listed as unknown sender (Spain), 1566), AAV Segr. Stato Spagna 1, DECODE R85–R90**: read end to end, 21 Sept 2026, with Lasry's key for the 1568 letters. See `targets/nuncio1566/`.
- **253 and 254 — Cardinal Alessandrino to the nuncio in Spain, 1568–69, AAV Segr. Stato Spagna 6/I and 6/II, DECODE R93–R102, R115**: read end to end, 21 Sept 2026, with George Lasry's reconstructed polyphonic key (two letters per digit) and a language-model decoder. See `targets/alessandrino1568/`.

- **224 — Johan Isaac de Swart (St Petersburg) to Van Bleiswijk and the States-General, 1782 and 1787, DECODE R1036/R1040**: the codebook DECODE could not identify is Croiset&rsquo;s 1765 key for the Russia legation (DECODE R1038). R1036 read at 93.8% of 1,719 groups. R1040 read in part: its second codebook segment reads with the same key, and the first 1,928 groups are in a missing codebook. See [targets/deswart1782/](targets/deswart1782/), removed 21 Sept 2026.

- **227 — Dirk van Hogendorp to Maarten van der Goes, St Petersburg, 5 July 1803, DECODE R1942**: subject identified from Sillem&rsquo;s 1890 account and its explicit citation of the ciphered dispatch no. 12. Hogendorp sought to bypass Chancellor Vorontsov and reach Emperor Alexander I through General Van Suchtelen. The matching 1803 codebook is DECODE R1035; the exact 286-group plaintext remains to be aligned. [Write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/r1942.html).

- **28 — Charles of Egmond → grand master of France, BnF fr. 3015 no. 8**: French body and address read with an inferred substitution key and nulls. Arnhem, apparently 18 July; no year established. Minor glyph doubts and literal anomalies retained. [Write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/egmond.html).

- **34 — Charles IX to Philibert du Croc, undated (1565–67 or 1572), DECODE R2789**: found already solved by George Lasry; two-page plaintext overlays published by Satoshi Tomokiyo on 25 May 2022. DECODE's “Non-decrypted” status is stale, and its 1 January 1550 display is not a document date: Destray's document XLIII is explicitly *S. l. n. d.* [Write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/charlesixducroc.html).

- **236 — Giovanni Battista Pallotto (Vienna), 70 ciphered despatches of 1628, BAV Barb.lat. 6956, DECODE R215–R285, R318**: solved by others. Norbert Biermann and Thomas Bosbach broke the key in March 2018; their keys and a decryption are filed as documents on DECODE R215 (only R215–R225 are marked "Partially decrypted"). Removed 21 Sept 2026. See [targets/pallotto1629/](targets/pallotto1629/).

- **237 — Monsignor Giovanni Battista Pallotto (Vienna) to Cardinal Barberini, 28 ciphered despatches, BAV Barb.lat. 6960, DECODE R286–R313 (4 Aug – 29 Nov 1629)**: the contents have been in print since 1897. Hans Kiewning, *Nuntiaturberichte aus Deutschland* IV/2, *Nuntiatur des Pallotto 1628–1630*, Bd. 2: *1629* (Berlin 1897) prints some forty of these despatches headed *dechiffr.*, from the copies deciphered in Rome (ASV Nunz. di Germania 119); the identification was confirmed against the register's own clear pages and its enclosure on p. 13. The cipher itself is not broken and no partial key is claimed (a held-out test rules the apparent one out); calibrated controls rule out every fixed-width hypothesis — a search of all 1,161,303 letters of the edition finds no window of four ciphertexts enciphering anything in print at width 2, 3 or 4 — and one positive finding identifies the system: 39 repeated 24-digit strings where chance gives none, which excludes real homophony — a code with deterministic variable-length groups. The sibling entry 236 (Barb.lat. 6956, Pallotto 1628, 70 ciphertexts) stays open, but Kiewning's Band 1 (1628) is the obvious place to look first. [Write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/pallotto1629.html). **Update 21 Sept 2026:** George Lasry had found years ago that 6960 uses the same key as 6956 (Biermann and Bosbach, 2018, on DECODE R215); applied here, it reads all 28 ciphertexts apart from ~1,750 nomenclator groups missing from that key.

- **160 — Beatrice d'Aragona (1482–1505)**: four earlier printed readings and one plain letter, verified 20 Sept 2026. March editorial markers remain unexplained. Split by key on 14 Oct 2026: [1482](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/beatrice1482.html), [March 1486](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/beatrice1486.html), [May 1486](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/beatrice1486b.html), [1505, not a cipher](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/beatrice1505.html).

DECODE R4282 was separated from entry 207 on 14 Oct 2026 after George Lasry recovered its key with CTTS; see [the standalone write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/decode4282.html). Entry 207 remains open for its other 11 records; its identifier is retained.

Entries read, partly read, resolved or found already in print in this repo were removed from the catalogue on 19 Sept 2026 (ids 1–6, 8, 14–16, 21, 22, 24, 26, 27, 32, 33, 149 and 159; ids are not reused; id 12, the Forget–Matignon–Mayenne leaves of BnF fr. 15572/15571, removed the same day and restored on 14 Oct 2026 narrowed to the unattempted Cipher-2 leaves ff. 218–219, its Cipher-1 and Cipher-3 leaves now the entries matignon1586 and matignon1586c3; id 9, BnF Espagnol 318, removed the same day and restored on 14 Oct 2026 narrowed to the unread nos. 93–94, its nos. 5, 92 and 95 now separate entries (federico1497, esp318, esp318no95); id 38, Carpio to Fuenmayor 1677, read and removed the same day; id 40, Balbases to Fuenmayor 1677–78, DECODE R985–R998, key rebuilt from the margins and all fourteen read (four never deciphered before), removed the same day; id 215, Frederick II to Michell 1751, found printed in the Politische Correspondenz and removed the same day; id 62, the TNA SP 106/10 worksheets R658–R702, shown to be the sp106box10 codebreaker's sheets (his grid rebuilds the Venetian three-figure code), removed 21 Sept 2026; id 56, the TNA SP 106/10 pigpen letter, DECODE R927, broken and read in part and removed the same day; id 243, Morosini to Montalto 1588–89, DECODE R18–R58, read with Meister's printed key and removed the same day (split on 14 Oct 2026: R53/R54, in a second key, are morosini1589, read in part with Lasry's partial key); ids 54, 55, 59 and 60, the Henrietta Maria circle letters of 1645–46 in TNA SP 106/10 ff. 207–247 (DECODE R752–R929), found already read by George Lasry, whose keys and plaintexts are on the DECODE records (2020), and removed the same day; see targets/hm1645/NOTES.md; id 67, Kurz von Senftenau to Trauttmansdorff 1639, DECODE R3811–R4736, key rebuilt from the R3811 glosses and all nine letters read in part, removed the same day; id 240, Acciaiuoli from Lisbon to the Secretariat 1758–60, DECODE R25, R195–R201, Lasry's letter stream read as Italian with 25 code words fixed from the leaves deciphered at the time, removed the same day). Their readings are in [SOLVED_CATALOGUE.md](SOLVED_CATALOGUE.md), on the site's [solved](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/solved.html) page and in the linked directories. Entry 7 now covers only the Ranzo letter to "Garbino" (fr. 3022 no. 20), the del Vasto items beside it being read; entries 7 and 20 were attempted here without a reading and stay in the open ranking. Entry 149 (Lope de Soria to Charles V, eleven ciphertexts, RAH 9/28) was read in part on 19 Sept 2026 and removed; see [targets/soria1523/](targets/soria1523/); ids 211 and 213, d'Affry's intercepted letters from The Hague 1757–58 (KHA Prins Willem V inv. 192, DECODE R1054–R1076, R2067), key rebuilt from Lyonet's decipherments found among the images and nine letters read, removed the same day (R1071, to Stainville, is in another code and stays unread; id 213 restored on 14 Oct 2026, narrowed to R1071, attempted, open, see [targets/affry1757c/](targets/affry1757c/)). Id 216, Kauderbach's intercepted despatches from The Hague 1754–56 (KHA Prins Willem V inv. 201, DECODE R1043–R2082), unseparated figures split and the key found against the vocabulary of his 1761 key (DECODE R936), all ten read, removed the same day; see [targets/kauderbach1754/](targets/kauderbach1754/). Id 248, Barberini to Nuncio Ceva 1632 (AAV Segr. Stato Francia doss. 346, DECODE R75, R77, R78, R82, R84), found to be three-fifths already read by George Lasry, whose reconstructed key of 2020 is on every record of the dossier; the two letters he had not deciphered, R75 and R84, were read here and removed the same day; see [targets/ceva1632/](targets/ceva1632/). Id 249, the Secretariat's ciphered despatches to the nuncio in Spain 1718–20 (ASV Segr. Stato Spagna 364D, DECODE R154–R177), found already solved and read by George Lasry, whose reconstructed key and segmented decipherment of the whole Spagna 364 corpus are attached to every one of the 24 records (24 Oct 2020); measured at 97.6 % read, and 61 nomenclator groups his key left without a value recovered here from the alphabetical runs and the corpus contexts; removed on 20 Sept 2026; see [targets/nunzio1718/](targets/nunzio1718/). Id 57, the seven anonymous Italian ciphertexts of TNA SP 106/10 (DECODE R657, R660, R664, R704, R720–R722), shown to be one Jacobean codebreaker's working file, dated from its own nomenclature to 1623–24, its cipher explained from the key he wrote out (DECODE R725, catalogued as a key and so outside the entry) and its three-figure code rebuilt far enough to read phrases; removed on 20 Sept 2026; see [targets/sp106box10/](targets/sp106box10/). Id 17, Henri IV to Maisse at Venice 1592–93 (BnF fr. 16093 ff. 370, 373, 406, 410), read: two ciphers, not one, each with a key Tomokiyo had published as an image and never applied, and f. 370 verified letter-for-letter against a clear copy in Brienne 13; removed on 20 Sept 2026; see [targets/maisse1592/](targets/maisse1592/). Id 19, the Spanish despatches of 1593 (BnF fr. 3983 nos. 45, 79 and fr. 3984 nos. 47, 68), read in part: the Spanish office's own key, with its nomenclature, found at BnF fr. 3995 f. 97 through the DECODE key records 4076-4077, and Tomokiyo's two key images confirmed against it; removed on 20 Sept 2026; see [targets/sessa1593/](targets/sessa1593/) (no. 68, in the Feria–Mansfeld cipher, split off on 14 Oct 2026 to [targets/ibarra1593/](targets/ibarra1593/)). Id 30, Laurière to Nevers, Châlons, 9 Jul 1593 (BnF fr. 3625 no. 55), kept uncounted after the first pass, read in full with his own key sheet, Tomokiyo's Nevers key no. 57 (identified by setsunaatto, issue 13, 29 Sept 2026); removed on 14 Oct 2026 when the Champagne entry was split by key; see [targets/lauriere1593/](targets/lauriere1593/), [targets/champagne1590/](targets/champagne1590/) and [targets/bienvenut1590/](targets/bienvenut1590/).

## Also noted, not counted

- **Riksarkivet key-box and Oxenstierna ciphers, 8 records (Bremen 1631, R4282, R4306 and Rusdorff 1625 read or partly read and removed)**, 1628 – 1633, Riksarkivet, TNA of Sweden (8 items), [DECODE R4284](https://de-crypt.org/decrypt-web/RecordsView/4284), [DECODE R4333](https://de-crypt.org/decrypt-web/RecordsView/4333), [DECODE R4334](https://de-crypt.org/decrypt-web/RecordsView/4334), +5 more records. DECODE R4284–R4341, 8 records in four different ciphers, each its own entry since the one-key split of 14 Oct 2026. Viewed 21 Sept 2026. The two Bremen letters of 1631 (R4330–R4331, riksarkivet1628.html) are read and removed from this entry; R4282 was deciphered by George Lasry (decode4282.html). R4306: contributed partial Dutch decipherment by George Lasry, transcribed with CTTS then solved with Opus 5.5 (17 Oct 2026; sweden4306.html, which also records this project's failed attempt of 21 Sept 2026); pencilled 1632? and Amsterdam attribution remain qualified, four code identities open. R4336–R4337 turned out to be Rusdorff's London letters of February 1625 in a homophonic cipher with an alphabetical nomenclator; key recovered from the ciphertext by George Lasry (21 Oct 2026, PR #72; rusdorff1625.html) and removed from this entry. Open: R4284 (Latin 1628, Camerarius docket; riksarkivet4284.html) — attacked with Latin annealing and against two box keys, not broken; R4333–R4335 (Rusdorff → Oxenstierna 1628, another cipher; rusdorff1628.html) and R4338–R4341 (E 708, Swedish, June 1633, not 1646; glossed in part at the time; oxenstierna1633.html) surveyed only. One remaining key-box ciphertext that resisted a first attack, and Oxenstierna correspondence of 1628 and 1633 with a key collection that may hold their keys. IoC 1.07, not a simple substitution; mono, periodic and Quagmire-type anneals failed. Needs the key or the sibling letters. Stato, Spagna 1A (IA-1; DECODE R91), [DECODE R91](https://de-crypt.org/decrypt-web/RecordsView/91), [DECODE R92](https://de-crypt.org/decrypt-web/RecordsView/92). DECODE R91: five cipher page images and four cleartext Farnese letters of summer 1542, downloaded and measured here 18 Sept 2026 (targets/vatican5/NOTES.md). 2,956 digits; a different key and design from IA-2: doubled digits at 0.88 of chance (IA-2 0.30), wide gaps not word division. No key sheet, no decipherment on the record. DECODE R1854 image 1 (the record holds four letters in four ciphers; images 2-5 deciphered here, now the entries r1854, mutiliana1428 and cattanei1430; this item is targets/malatesta1428/, split 14 Oct 2026). Three cipher runs, 262 letter-like signs (ſſ, η, ʒ, H, cc, ∩, I, ƀʒ ...) inside a clear Latin letter on the peace of Ferrara (April 1428). Not a simple or homophonic substitution in Latin or Italian (controls pass); the R1853 register's Malatesta keys do not fit. Pandolfo Malatesta's secret plans for his brother-in-law after the peace of Ferrara; one of the oldest unread cipher passages with images online. Imp. 1, solv. 2, diff. 3, class B, priority 2.1.
- **Letters to Mr Carré, Pall Mall, 1742–45, 3 ciphertexts**, Mar 1742 – 19 Jan 1745, BL, Add MS 32305 ff. 11–12, 14–17, 22–23 (3 items), [DECODE R2968](https://de-crypt.org/decrypt-web/RecordsView/2968), [DECODE R2970](https://de-crypt.org/decrypt-web/RecordsView/2970), [DECODE R2972](https://de-crypt.org/decrypt-web/RecordsView/2972). Viewed 21 Sept 2026 (targets/carre1742/, split on 14 Oct 2026 from bl32305, where it had been folded into item 78). R2968/R2970/R2972 (letters to Mr Carré, Pall Mall, Mar 1742, received 22 Aug 1742, 19 Jan 1745): transcribed in full (1323 groups, 381 distinct, 1–800); not a letter cipher (anneal = shuffled control), not one-part (r = −0.26): two-part code of ~800 entries, closed; R2969 is a frequency count, not a key. Three anonymous letters to a Mr Carré in Pall Mall, intercepted 1742–45, entirely in a code with no gloss; what they hide is not known. Imp. 2, solv. 1, diff. 4, class B, priority 1.6.
- **Trigram code fragment 'Suite du No 13 / No 24'**, undated, BL, Add MS 32280 f 3, [DECODE R8871](https://de-crypt.org/decrypt-web/RecordsView/8871). DECODE R8871, viewed 21 Sept 2026. The leaf is a ruled form in a French hand, 'Suite du No 13' / 'No 24', with 77 three-character groups (letters, digits, œ), 76 of them distinct and near-flat statistics. It is a large or enciphered code, too short to rebuild. None of the 26 DECODE key records of Add MS 32280 (R8890, R8894–R8919, 1790–1842, mostly Dutch numeric codes) fits. R8870 (ff. 1–2) is an unrelated numeric letter. Notes in targets/r8871/NOTES.md. An unidentified numbered trigram-code series copied by the Deciphering Branch; only No 24's tail is imaged. Imp. 1, solv. 1, diff. 3, class B, priority 1.2.
- **Intercepted letter from Brussels with three short number runs**, 3 Apr 1660 NS, Bodleian, MS Rawlinson A 67 (Thurloe papers vol. lxvii), p. 270; printed Thurloe State Papers vii 869, [source](https://www.british-history.ac.uk/thurloe-papers/vol7/pp860-869), [Cryptiana](https://cryptiana.web.fc2.com/code/thurloe.htm), [Cryptoole XXXXXX-1](https://cryptoole.satoru.net/case.html?id=XXXXXX-1). Cryptoole XXXXXX-1 (open). About 21 numbers in three runs (6, 10 and 5 figures, values 4-72), e.g. '61 50 57 62 72 66 54 61 68 60'; printed without decipherment. Reviewed 20 Oct 2026: all 21 numbers extracted from Birch VII p. 869 and screened against twelve existing transcribed key tables (research/catalogue_review_20261020/brussels_key_screen.json). No coherent reading identified; some tables are partial, so their families are not excluded. A royalist note from Brussels in the weeks before the Restoration; small but cheap to test. Imp. 1, solv. 1, diff. 3, class C, priority 1.2.
<!-- also:end -->

## Method

- Harvest: Gallica SRU `dc.description all "chiffre" and dc.type all "manuscrit"` → `research/gallica_sweep/sru_chiffre_desc.json`;
  items split on Omont's numbering and kept when they say *chiffre* without *déchiffrement*
  (`research/gallica_sweep/bnf_candidates.txt`). Shelfmarks resolved from IIIF manifests (`research/gallica_sweep/ark_shelfmarks.json`).
- Coverage: string search of correspondents against cryptiana `unsolved.htm` and the six French article pages saved
  in `research/gallica_sweep/src/`.
- DECODE refill: `research/catalogue_harvest/decode/dump_list.py` (record list), `dump_views*.py` (full records),
  `digest.py` (groups + same-series keys), `rule_score.py` (scores, merges, exclusions from `overrides.json`),
  `merge.py` (into `catalogue.json`). Cookie in `targets/bordeaux/decode/cookie.txt`, JWT from any RecordsView page.
- Not done: image inspection of ◇ entries; Tomokiyo's François I article; Scheurer, Savasse, Mousset editions;
  DECODE search for each item.

**Checked:** catalogue descriptions and item numbering; shelfmarks from the IIIF manifests; presence/absence of a *déchiffrement* item in the same
volume; fr. 3484 f. 34 on the image (cipher, no decipherment on the leaf); Tomokiyo's status wording for entries 11–14, 16–17, 19–21, 23–24; fr. 16127 on the image.
**Not checked:** the leaves of ◇ entries; printed editions that may contain clear texts; DECODE records.
**User must verify** before attacking any entry: open the ark at the item's folio and confirm no decipherment
follows, then search DECODE and Tomokiyo's site for the correspondent and date.
